# BLACKBOARD — w2m

*The project's working record. Seven sections. Single-writer per section. Everything written here that matters to the Architect was also said in chat in the same turn. Template: `sdd-kit-3/templates/BLACKBOARD.md`.*

---

## Surfaced for review

- **2026-10-08 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (after vocabulary bumps 0.2, 0.3, 0.4; hard rule 5, overdue since 0.2 per the project review): The vocabulary in force is `signals/0.4.json`, 60 tags in 12 categories on two planes. The harness plane is substrate events whose kind is the Struct class name; the guest plane is content-addressed JSONL traces ordered by (thread_id, thread_seq, hb_refs). Since 0.1: harness integers are bounded to ±(2^53−1) for RFC 8785 (P8); capability values name the 4 KB IPA granule, guest TSO, and three 4 KB protection paths (P9, P10); 0.4 renames `objective_ref` (no money word in an optimization target), adds `not_established` so a probe that cannot decide says so, and lets imported executions omit what was not observed, naming it in `unobserved`. What the project is for has not changed: contracts first, generated implementations verified against them, a Windows game launched as a Mac app (v0.2 §1). What has fired: 14 of 60 tags; none of generation, model checking, reduction, or discrepancy routing. That gap is the next work (Decision 2026-10-08, sequence).
- **2026-10-03 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION: This project is w2m, a system meant to take the Windows x86-64 distribution of a Steam game and produce a Mac-native application from the binaries alone, governed by a versioned, executable Windows execution contract (`windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_1.md` §2-3). The review at `docs/review-2026-10-03-research-direction-v0_1.md` narrows v0 to one DRM-free D3D11 game in a Hypervisor.framework VM backend using the macOS 26 4 KB IPA granule and hardware TSO, on this host (macOS 26.5.1, M5 Max, 16 KB host pages). SDD at root: the vocabulary is the contract, signals are designed typed events and not logs, and the lossy step being removed is a human describing why a game misbehaves — here the program's own trace (contract id, minimized program, mismatch class) replaces it, which is the research direction's §11 discrepancy report expressed as signals. The loop: write a card, self-review it, execute, run the gates (`sdd-kit-3/AGENTS.md` dual contract plus observation contract for anything that runs guest code), write the Signal Report, run the Rubber Duck Pass against `signals/0.1.json`, append to this file per single-writer discipline. Resolve-and-record: questions the project, the kit, or a named engineering source settle get decided and written to `## Decisions` with grounds; only the closed user-only list (here: which game, the Windows oracle hardware, Steam credentials, licensing intent) is asked, in chat, while independent work continues. No implementation sprint dispatches before `signals/0.1.json` and its rationale are signed (hard rule 12); `sprints/sprint-000-vocabulary-session.md` is that session.

- **2026-10-03 agent** — User-only questions asked in chat, each with a recommendation: (Q1) first target game; (Q2) Windows x86-64 oracle machine; (Q3) Steam credentials for DepotDownloader, deferred to the acquisition phase; (Q4) commercial intent, given substrate's PolyForm Noncommercial license. Sprint 0 proceeds on recommended answers marked provisional.

- **2026-10-03 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (re-articulated after the v0.2 scope change): The founding document is now `windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_2.md`. Its first-class subsystems are contract construction from three evidence categories (documented, observed on a named reference profile, hypothesis; §11.1), the specification compiler and the application compiler joined by one versioned contract (§2), code discovery with validity and invalidation (§5.1), the versioned Steam bridge (§4.2), and substrate as the orchestration harness whose append-only record does not serialize guest execution (§11.4). The plan leaves the CPU backend, first game, first graphics profile, and shader-converter adoption open, so this session's earlier VM-first and DRM-free calls no longer stand. Each becomes a recorded decision backed by capability probes and conformance results (§8.2, §15). Two repair paths stay separate: implementation repair against an unchanged contract, and contract revision with evidence and dependency invalidation (§11.3). The vocabulary has to carry both, so no candidate can make itself pass by changing its expected output. Sprint 0 (`sprints/sprint-000-vocabulary-session.md`) is retargeted to v0.2 and stays the next work.

- **2026-10-09 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: vocabulary 0.5 in force, the x86-64 profile changed, the architecture decided, 32-bit in scope; review 2026-10-09 progress K2): w2m runs a Windows x86-64 Steam game on this Mac inside a Hypervisor.framework VM that enforces x86 ordering and 4 KB pages in hardware. The guest's Windows-visible state (TEB, PEB, CONTEXT, a per-thread state block) lives in guest memory at Windows layouts; Windows services are freestanding C inside the guest, each a compilation unit generated against a contract and judged against Windows; x86 code is decoded by a spec-generated decoder judged by XED, lifted to explicit operations judged by a real x86 CPU, and compiled to AArch64. The x86-64 profile is Hail to the Rainbow (Unity 2019.4, Mono: its logic is code generated at run time); BioShock 2 Remastered is the 32-bit profile. Vocabulary 0.5 (61 tags, two planes) names every event; a substrate run holds the chain from cause to verdict, and the records under store/runs/ replay. The plan's current order lives in docs/plan.md.

- **2026-10-09 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: vocabulary 0.7 in force since Sprint 039; kit reread in full after compaction): w2m runs Hail to the Rainbow (Unity 2019.4, Mono, x86-64) on this M5 Max inside a Hypervisor.framework VM; x86 is decoded by a spec judged by XED, lifted to explicit operations judged instruction by instruction against Intel CPUs (39,708 cases in Sprint 039, faults included), and compiled to AArch64; Windows services are freestanding C in the guest. SDD gaps found in the reread and fixed in Sprint 095's commit: `## Built` stops at Sprint 066, and `## Sprint tail` holds about 60 entries against the kit's 10.

- **2026-11-25 agent (supervisor)** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION, vocabulary 0.13 (hard rule 5, after a vocabulary bump; `signals/rust-current.json`; 62 tags; P25-P29): w2m runs Hail to the Rainbow's Windows x64 build in a Hypervisor.framework VM from a Rust host, on step 6 of `docs/rust-port-plan-2026-10-24.md`, several vCPUs. 0.13 gives that step its words: a run's `vcpus` on EXECUTION_STARTED and MEASUREMENT_RECORDED (E14, at least 1, enforced by the next card's `NonZeroU64`); `locked_model_steps`, `turn_start_ns_p50/p99` (the turn's start including its wait for the runtime word); a `holder` on the four `runtime_word_*` metrics alone (E15, checked by `check_records`); and `feat_lse2`, `feat_bbm_level2`, `guest_wfe_exit` for the capability checks `vcpus_allowed` makes. SDD holds the vocabulary as the contract: no tag or value enters except by a typed proposal ratified on stated grounds, the version bumped and every pin found by one search; signals are the run's own account on a substrate record, not log lines. The loop stands as the user set it on 2026-11-24: the supervisor writes the card, a builder reads the whole kit and the card and stops at a gated tree, the supervisor reruns the contracts and plants, writes BLACKBOARD and makes one commit a sprint from 334. Next: the `--vcpus N` record with CAPABILITY_PROBED, then C1.

## Decisions

- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — What role substrate plays. Decision: substrate is the generation-and-verification harness (research direction §1 "the project's own generation and automated verification system"): contract implementations are generated and checked through `best_of_n_verified` / `coding_flow`-shaped topologies, and every probe, generation, and conformance run is a substrate run record. Substrate is not the compiler or the guest runtime. Grounds: project — research direction §1, §11 and substrate `docs/applications.md`; SDD — `ADDENDUMS.md` Addendum D (discovery harness built on substrate); practice — substrate `docs/api.md` (`embedded_substrate`, `assert_event`, `first_divergence`). Revisit if: the user meant substrate as code to fork, or as the shipped runtime.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — The Windows execution contract is mined from a Windows oracle by probes; Microsoft documentation is a hint, not the source of truth. Grounds: project — review §1; SDD — `grammar/BOOTSTRAP.md` anti-pattern "fabricating to avoid a gap"; practice — Wine project history (1993 to date) of undocumented-behavior dependence. Revisit if: a contract built from documentation alone passes a real game's observation contract.

- **2026-10-08 user** — Check the decoder approach against published theory and best practice before continuing ("appeal to that now... make sure we're on the right track", chat 2026-10-08).
- **2026-10-08 agent-resolved** — Decoder plan revised from published work (`docs/review-2026-10-08-decoder-theory.md`): (1) Intel XED becomes the primary reference decoder; Capstone and LLVM stay as additional voters; (2) disagreements are adjudicated by authority — CPU (windows-2025 runner) first, then XED and the SDM text — not by vote, because independently built decoders fail together (Knight & Leveson 1986) and w2m's spec shares sources with the references; (3) generated encodings per spec row join the corpus (mishegos, Paleari et al.); (4) operand comparison, then semantic comparison by execution (MeanDiff; Dasgupta et al. K semantics) follow length/mnemonic; (5) worklist items are labeled contract revision (spec row) or implementation repair (decoder routine), per v0.2 §11.3. Grounds: project — v0.2 §5, §11.1, §11.3; SDD — TECHNIQUES.md #5 (external check surface); practice — Paleari et al. ISSTA 2010; Woodruff et al. LangSec 2021; Knight & Leveson IEEE TSE 1986; Kim et al. ASE 2017; Dasgupta et al. PLDI 2019. Revisit if: XED and the CPU disagree (then the CPU decides and XED gets a recorded exception).
- **2026-10-08 agent-resolved** — Decoding depends on the CPU profile: the same bytes are TZCNT or BSF (BMI1), LZCNT or BSR, ENDBR64/RDSSP or NOP (CET), observed with XED chips PENTIUM4, SKYLAKE, ALL. Decision: the decoder takes the reference profile's CPU feature set (`features`, XED feature names); spec rows that depend on a feature carry `requires` and `fallback`; the XED reference is run with the matching chip. Grounds: project — v0.2 §11.1 (reference profile includes CPU identity and exposed features); SDD — TECHNIQUES.md #5; practice — Intel XED chip model (`xed_decoded_inst_set_input_chip`); SDM TZCNT/LZCNT compatibility notes. Revisit if: a game's behavior differs between reference CPUs with and without a feature (then the application profile pins the CPU).

- **2026-10-09 agent-resolved** — Runtime architecture (v0.2 §8.2 backend selection; review 2026-10-09 H1). Supersedes "Sprint 034 decides the guest runtime architecture" in the sequence entry above: the evidence was already on the record and leaves one design.
  (1) **Backend: the managed VM.** On this host, guest TSO in the VM runs at 0.99-1.10x native plain access, against 2.64-2.71x for the cheapest native-process lowering (ldapr/stlr) and 7.4x for dmb fences (Sprint 011, record ordering-1791442786); EnTSO gives 0 forbidden MP outcomes in 50M (Sprint 009); the VM enforces 4 KB guest pages in hardware (fault EC 0x25 at A+4096), while a macOS process cannot (mprotect at 4 KB returns EINVAL; Sprint 012); no public native-process TSO control exists in the SDK (Sprint 008). The VM back end already runs lifted code and passes lockstep and the state differential (Sprints 030, 032). Native-process lowering stays a later comparison subject (v0.2 §8.2), not a second build.
  (2) **Windows-visible state lives in guest memory at Windows layouts.** The game's runtime reads it there without calling anything: Mono's JIT reads TLS from `gs:[0x1480 + key*8]` and `gs:[0x1780]` (TEB), its VEH reads and rewrites CONTEXT, Boehm GC and Mono call SuspendThread, GetThreadContext and SetThreadContext. So TEB, PEB, the loader's module list, the VEH chain, and one CPU-state block per thread (GPRs, RIP, flags, XMM/YMM, MXCSR, x87 with its control word, segment bases) are guest memory. Translated code may hold guest registers in host registers; every block carries reconstruction tables (host PC → guest RIP and register locations) so a CONTEXT can be built at any instruction boundary (v0.2 §5).
  (3) **Windows services run inside the guest, as AArch64 code compiled from C** (freestanding, `clang --target=aarch64-none-elf`, no libc), linked into the guest runtime image the monitor loads. Faults, VEH dispatch, TLS, handles, waits, thread suspend and context access never leave the VM. Only host effects cross the boundary — files, time, windows, input, audio, graphics, network — through one typed transport (v0.2 §7, last paragraph), replacing today's text pipe to Python. C, because clang and the AArch64 toolchain are on this host (Xcode 26.6), the monitor and probes are already C, the Windows ABI and its structures are C-shaped, and the reference implementations the project measures against are C (Wine, box64). Every service is a separate compilation unit with one contract, so the candidate loop generates, installs and judges it the way Sprint 031 did SetLastError; Python candidates end with the host kernel32.
  (4) **One vCPU per guest thread**, each on its own host thread (Hypervisor.framework binds a vCPU to the thread that created it); macOS schedules them. SuspendThread forces an exit with `hv_vcpus_exit` and reads the state block; guest TSO holds across them (EnTSO set per vCPU, Sprint 008).
  (5) **Translation**: x86-64 → explicit operations (`lift.py`) → AArch64 (`vm_backend.py`), ahead of time for shipped code and on a dispatch miss for code found or written at run time (v0.2 §6). The translator stays Python on the host behind one interface (guest bytes and address in; AArch64 block, its source range, code version and reconstruction table out), so moving it into the guest later changes no caller. Mono's RWX regions are write-protected after translation; a write faults, invalidates the affected translations, and the next entry retranslates (v0.2 §5.1).
  (6) **Signals**: substrate orchestrates generation and judgment on the harness plane, as now. The guest runtime writes guest-plane events (vocabulary 0.5) into a guest ring buffer the monitor drains to the trace store, so every service call, fault and thread event is judged by the existing comparator. Translation emits `CODE_TARGET_DISCOVERED`, `TRANSLATION_PUBLISHED` and `TRANSLATION_INVALIDATED`, which have no emitter today.
  Grounds: project — v0.2 §5, §5.1, §6, §7, §8.1, §8.2; Sprints 008, 009, 011, 012, 030-033 measurements; Unity-Technologies/mono `unity-2019.4-mbe` source (read 2026-10-09); SDD — AGENTS.md resolve-and-record, hard rule 4; practice — box64 and Wine keep Windows structures in guest-visible memory and write their runtimes in C; Rosetta 2 and FEX reconstruct x86 state from host state at fault points. Revisit if: a native-process TSO control appears in a public SDK, or the transport's measured round trip makes a host effect miss its frame budget.

Each crossing is an exit over the pipe (10.3–10.5 µs, Sprint 048) plus the Python behind it, and none is measured as a share of a run: plan row 065 now measures the host's share by operation beside the model steps' share, from `vm.host_requests` and a time per operation. - **2026-10-13 user** — The game's call stream judges values strictly and presence only as far as Windows pins it (chat 2026-10-13: "we don't need to; we need to find the general shape. We don't need to find all the very specific variations ... it might be like overfitting"). Decision: a divergence is sorted before it is chased. A VALUE divergence (the same call on the paired thread with the same inputs, answered differently) is a defect. A presence or count divergence (a call one side makes and the other does not, or makes a different number of times) on a thread whose earlier calls all conform is SCHEDULING: the game's own interleaving, recorded and not chased; it is a defect only after an earlier value divergence explains it. HeapReAlloc 3268 (Unity's BatchDeleteObjects growing its buffer three times in runner run 38203364352 and four in the others) is scheduling. w2m is not tuned to reproduce one Windows execution's timing; Sprint 066's TIME_SLICE 20,000 stands on its own grounds or is revisited, not on matching run 38216081440's SList flushes. The general shape comes from the specification end: each function's contract (documentation, Wine and ReactOS as implementations, probe rows on Windows), judged by the families; the game's stream supplies coverage and catches value errors. The laptop's order follows: play mode (frames on the real GPU), audio, and probes first; batch-mode game series last. The sizes follow how far the game's threads got first: on 856c0e50 the batch run conforms at --clock-rate 1386000000 and diverges at 1400000000 (0x5b000, 0x83000) and 1414000000 (0x73000, 0x499000); 78d5b7d5's lowerings changed no value (a native-against-model differential of CVTTPS2DQ and CVTTPD2DQ on 6,018 blocks agreed) but ended model steps whose resumed blocks counted the rest of their block twice, which had moved the instruction clock. - **2026-10-14 agent-resolved** — The form of the compiler inside the VM (recentering step 6). Decision: copy-and-patch at the encoder level. A stencil is a sequence of arm64 encoder calls whose arguments are expressions of the instruction's guest registers and values; the stencils come from the Python compiler (the rules and `compile_insn`), captured per form and value class by the census's method, never written by hand; the compiler in the VM is a generated C decoder (from `specs/x86_decode.json`), the form key, the stencil table, the encoders in C (judged against `a64_encoder.py` by a differential) and the hole expressions; a form with no stencil exits to the host, which compiles it in Python as now and counts it. The words of every instruction compiled in the VM must equal the Python compiler's, over the census record and the class runs. Grounds: project — Sprint 071's census (`scripts/stencil_census.py` over frontier-643a6283510c record of the batch run to 100 billion instructions, cold): 1,313,377 compiled instructions, every one rebuilt exactly from its logged encoder calls; with value classes (zero, sign, the 12-bit and 9-bit ranges, the scaled offset per access size, nonzero halfwords, a shift count's 1 and 0) all 18,218 forms produce one encoder sequence; 1,287 distinct sequences, the commonest 100 covering 93.2% of compiled instructions; every hole a register, a value, a piece of one, its negation or an affine function of one (70 cases: 16 times an XMM number, 64 or 32 less a shift count). Industry — Xu and Kjolstad, "Copy-and-Patch Compilation", OOPSLA 2021 (1,666 stencils for WebAssembly); CPython 3.13's JIT, PEP 744, whose stencils are built from the same definitions as its interpreter, so one change updates both. Open, for the next sprints: 394,155 words (in 364,521 instructions) are built inline in `vm_backend.py` rather than by an encoder (ADD/SUB immediates, the flag sequences), 1,833 of their positions holding holes; they move into encoder functions first, with the words unchanged. The census covers the start-up's first 100 billion instructions; forms first met later (mono's run-time code in play) take the host path until a stencil exists for them.
- **2026-10-14 agent-resolved** — The stencil key of the compiler inside the VM, and how its holes are found (refines the copy-and-patch Decision above). Decision: a stencil is keyed by what the generated decoder gives (the spec row, iclass, operand and address size, VEX.L, the operands with register numbers and values taken out, which registers are equal, which explicit register is one of the row's implicit registers, the value classes of the immediates, displacements, the next address and any named address) and the compile call's flag arguments; the C decoder therefore outputs `x86dec.Fields` and `x86ops` operands, and a generated table gives each row's implicit registers. Keys come from recorded runs; each key's holes and their expressions come from compiling instances made for that key (registers and values changed within their classes), not from a run's variation. Grounds: project — Sprint 074 (`scripts/stencil_census.py --decoder-key` over a cold batch run's record of 1,313,377 compiled instructions): 38,154 keys, every one one encoder sequence; every hole that varies named by one expression of one decoded value over all its form's instructions; 17,276 keys seen with one distinct instruction, so the record leaves their holes unshown; the first key without implicit registers split a MUL row (MUL RDX). Practice — copy-and-patch derives each stencil's holes from the stencil's construction, not from observed uses (Xu and Kjolstad, OOPSLA 2021: a stencil's holes are the relocations in its object code, known from how the stencil was built). Revisit if: a key gives more than one sequence on a made instance.
- **2026-10-15 agent-resolved** — How the stencil table holds where its samples are thin (amends Decision 2026-10-14, the stencil key; review 2026-10-15 M4). Decision: (1) a key whose made instances give two sequences is refined by the first feature of one decoded value that separates them (at least 8, 16, 32, 64 or 128; the low 1-5 bits), from a per-key entry (Sprint 076, 58 keys); (2) the key's flag arguments are canonical: `live_after` and `nzcv_out` are kept only for an instruction with a flags op and `nzcv_from` only for a Jcc its kind's NZCV_CONDITIONS holds, since compile_insn reads them nowhere else (Sprint 079: 38,154 keys to 21,182, none split; the review's E2: 199,826 non-canonical argument sets, 0 differing words); (3) the table emits only what its samples prove: every value the samples never varied is guarded (an instruction whose value there differs goes to the host), a register hole is read only through the register map, and a value hole that a second expression also fits is checked at emit time, the instruction refused where they disagree (Sprint 080). Grounds: project — Sprint 079's held-out judge found 144 wrong compiles on 078's table, where a constant stood for a value no sample varied; Sprint 080's measures; SDD — KIT_DIARY #58, #59; practice — none outside the project for inferred stencils (copy-and-patch knows its holes by construction). Revisit if: a judge from outside the table's sources finds a differing word.

- **2026-10-23 agent-resolved** — How Rust w2m reaches substrate with no Python of its own. Every tag outside the `guest` category is a substrate application event: a frozen msgspec Struct emitted by a Producer, a Python callable `(input) -> AsyncIterable[Event]`, registered with `producer_kind(schemas=...)` on a `TopologyBuilder` that Python code builds (substrate `docs/api.md` lines 50-63, 280; `signals/0.9.json` `transport.harness_plane`). Substrate has no producer that runs another process, and `substrate run` takes only topologies registered in Python (its CLI; bundles describe chat sessions, not topologies). Decision: substrate gains, on its `w2m` branch, two language-neutral features: (1) a process producer that runs a command, reads one JSON event a line from its standard output (`{"kind": ..., "payload": {...}}`), checks each against schemas read from a JSON file (built as msgspec Structs at load), and emits it, a malformed line ending the producer as failed; (2) a topology read from a file: its producer kinds (each a command and its schema file), initial producers, triggers by kind, and termination by substrate's named recipes, run by `substrate run --file`. w2m's Rust writes the schema file from `signals/<version>.json` and its topology files; no Python file is added to w2m. Rejected: a thin Python adapter in w2m (new Python, Decision 2026-10-23 user); Rust writing substrate's record format itself (a second implementation of its sequencer, CRC framing and hashing, which substrate's replay would then have to trust). Grounds: project — the two gaps read in substrate's API and CLI; SDD — Decisions 2026-10-22 user (substrate stays) and 2026-10-23 user (no Python in w2m); practice — one implementation of a record format, reached across a process boundary by a line protocol, as the guest plane already reaches it (`transport.guest_plane`: a JSONL trace ingested by an operator). Revisit if: substrate's maintainers (the user) want the features elsewhere than the `w2m` branch, or a record from a process producer fails substrate's replay.

RustSec's advisory database has no entry for any of the six (`advisory-db/crates`, read 2026-10-24). Read in its source (the .crate from static.crates.io): an integer is kept exact as u64 or i64 and becomes f64 only past those ranges (`de.rs` `parse_integer`), which holds the guest plane's 64-bit values; a duplicate key keeps the last value (`value/de.rs` `visit_map`), which substrate's records cannot carry (RFC 8785); nesting stops at 128 (`remaining_depth`); objects are a `BTreeMap`, so key order is sorted, and every order the generator depends on comes from a JSON array. Grounds: project — the vocabulary generator (Sprint 126) and every judge of step 2 read JSON (`signals/`, substrate records, `specs/golden/`); SDD — WORKING_AGREEMENT permitted dependencies (a new one is a recorded decision first); practice — a hand-written parser is where JSON code fails (surrogate pairs, number edges), and serde_json's parser is the one read above. Revisit if: a judge needs integers past 64 bits exact (`arbitrary_precision`), or typed decoding enough to want `serde_derive`, each its own decision.

- **2026-10-24 agent-resolved** — Architecture: the Rust host's locks (Sprint 130; fix (b) of Decision 2026-10-24 user), in `docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-24.md`, which replaces `docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-23.md`. Decision: no thread waits, on any service path, for a lock another thread's service holds. (1) Each service the guest runtime answers takes the lock of what it touches or none: none for the thread-local calls and QueryPerformanceCounter (the three hottest imports, about 154,000, 129,000 and 94,000 calls a second in the batch run), a lock per heap as Windows has, the TLS slots, the handle table, each sync object, the modules, the call trace's atomic sequence, and the run-table word for the run table only, a leaf. (2) A transport page per vCPU, and no guest lock across `hvc #8`; each of the 65 `w2m_host` call sites is read for state it holds across the request. (3) Translation into a region per vCPU, with its own cursor, save area and runtime stack; dispatch slots read and written as atomic pairs or under a sequence word, filled by compare-and-swap, removed by tombstone; a lock per guest code page for invalidation; reclamation by per-vCPU epochs. (4) One process's objects and waits stay in its VM; the supervisor holds only what crosses processes. (5) Every lock word has Drepper's three states, a bounded spin, then a wait in the host on the word's address. Host locks are each held for one access, `files` only for the descriptor table; `modules` across one map, as Windows's loader lock is. The run-table word stays one lock for scheduling, about 31,000 takings a second in play, until the scheduler's port. Grounds: project — `vm_backend.py` 3762-3765, 3809, 4240-4251; `runtime.c` 25; Decision 2026-10-09 (hot imports); the reviewer's two passes; SDD — AGENTS.md hard rule 4; practice — QEMU's MTTCG (commit 0ac20318ce16, `docs/devel/multi-thread-tcg.rst`), crosvm's per-device mutex, Wine's esync to ntsync, Microsoft's HeapCreate and loader-lock pages, Drepper's "Futexes Are Tricky", Uhlig et al. (VM '04), the Arm ARM's concurrent-modification rule, Intel's cross-modifying-code rule, Go's per-P run queue. Revisit if: step 6 measures the run-table word busy more than 5% of the time or a p99 wait above 10 µs (then per-vCPU run queues), or a play stretch sends the supervisor more than 376 requests a second (then shared-page cross-process objects).

- **2026-10-31 agent-resolved** — Architecture: a vCPU with no thread (`docs/design-run-loop-on-several-vcpus-2026-10-31.md`, "A vCPU with no thread"; Sprint 318). Decision: that form carried: a vCPU with no thread it may take parks (`Switch::Parked`), its thread put down, `waiting` counted, the word and the guard let go, on a `Sched` condition beside `GuestLock` with the earliest deadline as its timeout; `THREAD_WAKE` and `threads::set_status`, the host's one writer of a thread's status, notify it; `slice_until` ends a slice only for ready threads beyond the waiting vCPUs; a run of several vCPUs is deadlocked only with every vCPU parked a second with no deadline and no notify; SuspendThread of a thread held elsewhere kicks its vCPU and waits; a process with a machine link is refused on several vCPUs, by name, until the Machine has its own thread (card 4f). Grounds: project — the form ran the threads and wait families on two vCPUs in the Python host, and the C runtime's half is already in the image; SDD — decided from the built prior form and the code before any second thread runs; practice — a vCPU with nothing to run waits in the host and is told, as QEMU's `hvf_wfi` returns a WFI to the host (the Python design's Sprint 121 note), never spins. Revisit if: a host service readies a thread outside `set_status`, or a wake must come from outside the guest's own serves.

- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 301 (the pre-commit checks a script). `scripts/pre-commit.sh`, `/bin/sh` with `set -eu`, runs the checks before a work commit in order (format, lint with the gates' flags, the Linux check, `design_check`, the record check, the doc tests) and stops at the first that fails, its status the failing check's; WORKING_AGREEMENT's rule opens with it.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 302 (the served part gathered). `Guest`'s 22 fields that a served exit writes are one value, `Guest.served: Served`, and `the_served_part_is_the_designs` holds them to the run-loop design's table.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 303 (BBM read; a test binary a file again for `hypervisor`). A vCPU reads `ID_AA64MMFR2_EL1` = 0x1001001102001011, BBM 0, so a block split needs break-before-make, a test holding the design's precondition 4 to it; `hypervisor`'s tests went back to one binary a file, Hypervisor.framework allowing one VM a process.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 304 (a block split stops the others and flushes). A valid block made a table at run time is written whole before its descriptor, and the requesting vCPU resumes through `FLUSH_STUB` (`dsb ishst; tlbi vmalle1is; dsb ish; isb; hvc #15`), which invalidates every vCPU's TLB; the batch run splits 35 blocks, `hvc 15` 35 in its held stop file. Sprint 305 replaced the order with the Arm one: the block invalid, the flush, then the table.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 305 (a split in the Arm order). A block split makes the block invalid, flushes every vCPU's TLB by running the requesting vCPU from inside the split through `FLUSH_STUB` until `hvc #15` (`run::flush_through_stub`, its registers, PSTATE and PC given back), then writes the table and its descriptor; the flush is passed down to the split's callers.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 306 (one VM at a time under `cargo test`). Every `w2m_vm` test that makes a VM holds `tests/all.rs`' `ONE_VM` for its life, so `cargo test` on this Mac exits 0, Hypervisor.framework allowing one VM a process; WORKING_AGREEMENT 142 says so.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 307 (the flush's registers and a pending cancel). `flush_through_stub` on a stopped vCPU gives back x0 to x30, CPSR and the PC as set, each a value of its own, and runs the stub again when a cancel is pending, noting it for after the split; `tests/flush_stub.rs` holds both.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 308 (the second vCPU's refusals). `w2m-vm --vcpus N`, 1 by default, refuses more than one by name before a run: FEAT_LSE2 not reported (`hypervisor::feature`, by `sysctlbyname`), a diagnostic that reads every exit, a child process, and a second vCPU not yet built.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 309 (how the vCPU threads share the guest). A design and a Decision: the whole `Guest` behind one lock, taken once per served exit inside the runtime word, the 42 functions keeping `&mut Guest`, each vCPU thread its own copy of the Load facts and its own view.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 310 (the guest behind one lock). `GuestLock` holds the run's borrow of `Guest`; a served exit reaches it through `serve()`, which asserts the runtime word first, the word taken before the lock and let go after it; the lock is `Sync`, a compile-time assertion holding it.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 311 (the run's shared state behind the lock). `GuestLock` holds the guest, the host compiler and the eight records a served exit or the in-VM compiler's drain writes; the drain and the exit's count take the word, then the lock; `turn` lets the guard go, then the word, around a process's wait for its turn; the guards of shared state run in release builds.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 312 (each vCPU's own pages, decided). A design and a Decision: each vCPU past the first its own level-0 table and private chain to its 18 pages (the monitor data page, the state block, the runtime stack) at vCPU 0's guest addresses, Sprint 103's form B carried; no guest code changed.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 313 (the host's per-vCPU base). The host reads a vCPU's monitor data page and state block through `VcpuPages`, the served exit's set by `serve(pages)` as the run's lock is taken, no fixed offset left (`vcpu_pages`' source test); vCPU 0's pages are the layout's, so one vCPU runs as before.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 314 (the entries made before a second vCPU). A design amendment, "The copied tables after load": no run-time descriptor writer writes a table a private chain copies under the roots 0xFE and 0xFF, so no table is made ahead, and `page_descriptor`'s refusal guards the rest; no code changed.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 315 (each vCPU's private chain). `Guest::private_chain(k)` gives vCPU `k` its own monitor data page, state block and stack at vCPU 0's guest addresses, under its own level-0 table and copies of the tables on the walk (two vCPUs read 0xA0A0 and 0xB1B1 at one address); `page_descriptor` refuses a descriptor in the copied ranges, and the instruction clock is refused on more than one vCPU.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 316 (the host's switch on any vCPU). `threads::switch` passes by a ready thread another vCPU holds and claims the thread it loads with the served vCPU's own `LOCK_OWNER`, as the C runtime's `held_elsewhere` does; on one vCPU the owner is 1, as before.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 317 (the commit owned by its checks). `scripts/commit.sh` refuses a working tree that differs from the index, runs `scripts/pre-commit.sh`, and commits only when the checks passed on the index they judged; WORKING_AGREEMENT's "Before a work commit" opens with it, closing the Drift watchlist's commit-chaining entry (seven commits of Sprints 312 to 315 joined to their checks by `;`).
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 318 (a vCPU with no thread, decided). A design section and a Decision: the Python host's park, timed wait, wake, deadlock over every vCPU, kick and Machine thread (its Sprints 118 to 122) carried into the run-loop design, card 4c3b split; a process with a machine link refused on several vCPUs until card 4f, so G3 waits on it; no code.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 319 (a vCPU parks and is woken). `Sched`, outside the run's lock, counts changes and parked vCPUs; `threads::switch` answers `Switch::Parked` when nothing a vCPU may take is ready while another vCPU holds a thread, the thread put down and the vCPU counted in the run table's `waiting`; `take` loads a thread onto a vCPU with none; `set_status` and `THREAD_WAKE` notify. One vCPU never parks.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 320 (how a parked vCPU's wait ends). `Sched::park` ends at a change, at the earliest blocked thread's deadline (`park_deadline`), or in a deadlock of the whole run, set in the shared counts and broadcast, after a second with every vCPU parked and none with a deadline; the wait takes no timeout of its own, the run's stop notifying it.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 321 (a slice ends for a thread no vCPU will take). `slice_until` counts only the ready threads this vCPU may load and ends the slice only when they are more than the vCPUs waiting, the Python host's `_slice_until`; one vCPU as before. CI on Sprint 320's close had failed `no_wall_clock_bound`, a whole-tree rule the crate-selecting gates did not run; the upper time bounds came out, and every whole-tree source test running on every change became Sprint 322.
- **2026-10-31** — Sprint 322 (the tree-wide rules run on every change). The tests that read beyond their own crate, listed in `scripts/tree-tests.txt`, run in every gates run that runs Rust tests, `scripts/test-filter.sh` the one place the gates' filter is built and a test holding the list whole; WORKING_AGREEMENT gains the rule that a command's description names what it changes.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 323 (the docs readers run on a docs change). The three tests that read the repository's `docs/` or `BLACKBOARD.md` from its root, in `id_registers.rs`, `census.rs` and `guest_parts.rs`, are listed in `scripts/tree-tests.txt` and so run on every change; `every_tree_test_runs_on_every_change` in `tree_tests.rs` holds the list to the files that read beyond their crate.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 324 (the run loop takes the lock as a thread would). The one-vCPU run loop moved into `vcpu_loop(lock: &GuestLock)` and reaches `Guest` through `serve` under the runtime word, its five whole-lock `get_mut` borrows gone, so the compiler refuses a mutable borrow there; the turn's start, measured at a median 375 ns against an exit's 1,709 ns over 54,388 turns, kept its first form.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 325 (a Machine is refused on several vCPUs). `Start` carries the run's vCPU count, and a child process's spawn is refused by name, `MACHINE_ON_ONE`, above one vCPU before its child or its link to the supervisor is made; the refusal's second plant waits on card 4c3b2b, the first run that can start two vCPUs.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 326 (the run makes its vCPU on a thread of its own). The run makes its one vCPU on a thread inside `std::thread::scope`, taking the `Vm` and the time limit from its three callers; the limit's timer waits on a channel the run's thread closes at its end, so it cannot outlive its run and stop a later vCPU given the same id.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 327 (a second vCPU runs the process), closed partial. A run of `start.vcpus` vCPU threads, vCPU `k` on its private chain with `LOCK_OWNER` `k + 1`, a vCPU with no thread parking until it takes one, the first stop the run's with every other vCPU stopped; `qpc`, `sleeper` and `twoready` end on two vCPUs as on one, and `threads::save` notifies a thread it readies. Partial for `statsize`, whose stat the host wrote into vCPU 0's stack, met at Sprint 328.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 328 (the host reaches a vCPU's own pages by address). The host resolves a guest address as the served vCPU sees it, its monitor data page, state block and runtime stack its own: `Layout::offset` takes the vCPU's pages and `Guest::offset` the served vCPU's, through 42 calls; `statsize` on two vCPUs through `w2m-vm` reads its own stat, "done".
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 329 (a stop found at the word's take). A take of the runtime word in a stopped run ends its service before it reads what the word guards, `take_word` returning None and giving the word back; an ended vCPU's guest's hold is let go on every path out of its thread, `HaltAtEnd`'s `Drop` after the halt, a panic included; `owner_of(k)` the one source of a vCPU's `LOCK_OWNER`.
- **2026-11-01** — Sprint 330 (the kick). SuspendThread of a thread another vCPU holds stops that vCPU through its handle and waits, the word and the lock let go, until the thread is put down or its vCPU leaves guest code; a cancel without the run's stop is a kick, and the kicked guest gets its word and its answer back; the threads probe's suspended spinner on two vCPUs stops before SuspendThread returns.
- **2026-11-24** — Sprint 331 (every other vCPU stopped for a locked model step). A model step of a form x86 locks (`translator::lower::is_locked`) cancels every other vCPU through its handle and waits it out of guest code before the step's load, resuming them after its store; `probes/locked_race` on one vCPU and on two loses no add, every row Windows run 41326239822's, its 100,000 locked steps counted.
- **2026-11-24** — Sprint 332 (the runner checks a binary's signature once). `scripts/sign_and_run.sh` stamps `$binary.signed` with the binary's modification time to the nanosecond (`stat -f %Fm`) and skips `codesign -d` when it matches, bash 3.2's whole-second `-nt` refused; a signed binary's launch went from 9.32-30.02 s to 0.00-0.01 s, the gates' test stage from 2,421 s to 1,136 s.
- **2026-11-25** — Sprint 333 (a sprint closes in its own commit). From Sprint 334 a sprint's work and its close are one "Sprint N: " commit whose tree holds card N done or closed-partial: `signal_report`'s `close_commit` and `trees_moved`, `scripts/gates-base.sh` and `scripts/pre-commit.sh` take it, a "Sprint N closed:" commit from 334 named; a sprint that adds a file under `store/binaries/` without changing the walk fixture is refused by name.
- **2026-11-25** — Sprint 334 (each gates step timed). One `timed NAME COMMAND...` helper in `scripts/gates.sh` prints each step's seconds and keeps a row a step in `store/gates/steps.tsv`, the run's unplaced seconds printed and kept; the run's time placed in nextest before its first test, 470 s, and `target/debug/deps`' 857,194 entries; the first sprint closed in its work commit.
- **2026-11-25** — Sprint 335 (the test build keeps no object files). `[profile.dev] split-debuginfo = "packed"` in the workspace `Cargo.toml` keeps each binary's debug information in one `.dSYM`: `target/debug/deps` went from 859,305 entries with 857,026 `.rcgu.o` to 300 with none, `codesign -d` on a test binary from 7.91 s to 0.00 s, and the gates' --all run from 1,024 s to 387 s.

(a) The host's take, `monitor_code::host_take_runtime_word`, spins without reading the run's stop, so a word left held makes the run outlive its limit (plant 1: no end in 120 s against a 60-s limit); the run-level test bounds itself at 120 s for it. (b) A service that panics now releases the host's hold as it unwinds, before the run's stop is set, so a peer may serve one exit on state the panic left half written before it finds the stop; the panic still fails the run at the scope's join. Re-visit: C5, the waiters that stop spinning, for both; (b) also if a run's service ever panics in a gate.

Re-visit: the next card that touches that block, which simplifies it and keeps the run's stop the labeled block's `Err(Stop)` carries.

## Open questions

- **OQ-1.** Is `ACTLR_EL1.TSOEN` settable for a guest vCPU through public Hypervisor.framework on macOS 26, or only through a private path? Settled by a probe sprint, not by reading. — **Answered 2026-10-07 (Sprint 008):** public. `HV_SYS_REG_ACTLR_EL1` (macOS 15.0+, SDK header `hv_vcpu_types.h`: "only allows getting / setting of the ACTLR_EL1.EnTSO bit (index 1)"); on Mac17,7 / macOS 25F80 the bit sets, reads back 0x2, and survives an `hv_vcpu_run` exit. Still open: the ordering *effect* (a two-vCPU litmus test). — **Effect demonstrated 2026-10-07 (Sprint 009):** MP litmus, 2 vCPUs, guest MMU on with Normal WB memory: EnTSO off 2245 / 50,000,000 forbidden outcomes; EnTSO on 0 / 50,000,000 (95% upper bound 6.0e-08).
- **OQ-3.** (Answered with OQ-1: the custom-guest path is public Hypervisor.framework, not a Virtualization.framework/Rosetta-only facility.) v0.2 §8.1 cites Apple's "Accelerating the performance of Rosetta" for guest `ACTLR.TSOEN` control. That page describes Rosetta inside a Linux guest under Virtualization.framework; whether a custom compact guest under Hypervisor.framework gets the same control is OQ-1, settled by the capability probe v0.2 §8.1 calls for.
- **OQ-2.** Which Metal Shader Converter version and license terms apply to redistributing its output inside a generated app bundle?

---
- **2026-10-08 (Sprint 031)** — `windows.thread.last_error.set_get@0.1.0` accepts a candidate whose GetLastError returns the constant 1234: its one scenario fixes the value at build time. What scenario and relation would make the value unpredictable to a candidate (a run-time value recorded in the trace, judged as "GetLastError returns what SetLastError was given")? Pinned by `test_one_scenario_contract_accepts_a_constant`.
- **2026-10-09 (Sprint 032)** — Answered for the Sprint 031 question above: version 0.2.0 picks three values at run time (QueryPerformanceCounter into a 256-entry table that carries each value's args_sha256) and judges with `exact` (GetLastError returns the argument of the latest SetLastError, checked by hash). The constant is rejected under 0.2.0; `test_constant_rejected_under_0_2_0_accepted_under_0_1_0` holds both verdicts.
- **2026-10-09 (substrate, upstream)** — `substrate.api.Producer` declares its events as `substrate.types.Event` (the bus frame), while the kernel takes and checks the producer's own msgspec Structs. A fix belongs in laffeyp/substrate (`AsyncIterable[Struct]`); w2m bridges it in `w2m.record.producer` until then.
- **2026-10-10 (review 2026-10-10 D8)** — The local VM needs internet when it runs the Steam client so `SteamAPI_Init` succeeds (Decision 2026-10-09, the VM's role), while the guest's lack of internet is "not a defect" for driving it (user, chat 2026-10-09). No sprint has needed Steam in the VM yet (local-VM review L3 open). Raise with the user when one does.
- **2026-10-16 (Sprint 089)** — Does lift() address through R8D-R15D under the 0x67 prefix with 64 bits? `_operand` sets 32-bit addressing only for an E-named base (or an E-named index with no base); the SDM (Vol 1 3.3.7) computes any 32-bit address-size effective address in 32 bits. The class case sets hold 11,202 such cases, 944 with the base's upper half nonzero, and their classes are accepted against Intel's results: evidence against, not proof (both addresses may fault, or reach the same bytes). Settled by examining those 944 cases' accesses, or by a probe on the laptop's x86 processor.

- **2026-10-16 (full review, item 6)** — Milestone C's bound. The review marks plan.md's "frames under 16.7 ms" as stale; the paste cut the text that replaces it. What is milestone C's speed bound (the game's own 113 frames a second on the laptop, 60, or another figure)? — **Answered 2026-10-16 (user):** no bound; as fast as possible, already the user decision of 2026-10-14.
- **2026-10-29 (the defect sweep)** — Which module hosts an `api-ms-win-core-*` set the table does not list? `modules.rs` 351-353 (modules.py 22) give every such set to kernelbase from one Windows row (synch-l1-2-0), while `API_SET_HOSTS` gives api-ms-win-core-com-l1-1-1 to combase.dll, so api-ms-win-core-com-l1-1-0 is likely combase's too. Settled by a LoadLibraryExW and GetModuleFileNameW row per set from the Windows runner, not by asking.
- **2026-10-29 (the threads.rs sweep)** — What stack does Windows give a created thread whose CreateThread commit equals the image's reserve? UnityPlayer.dll's call at reference row 2683 asks dwStackSize 0x100000, the exe's SizeOfStackReserve; Microsoft's Thread Stack Size page makes the reserve the commit rounded up to 1 MB, which would leave no guard page, so the sentence is not the whole answer. Both hosts reserve 0x100000 and clamp the commit to 0x100000 less 5 pages (threads.rs `create_thread`, vm_backend 3111-3112). Settled by a thread_stacks probe row from the Windows runner for a commit equal to the reserve and one above it, not by asking.
- **2026-10-29 (Sprint 218; reviewer-3's Medium 2)** — What TLS index does Windows give a DLL loaded after the start when a DLL imported at the start already holds index 1? The game's case: UnityPlayer.dll holds 1, and steam_api64.dll takes 2 on both hosts by the frozen host's rule (vm_backend 3959). `contracts/windows.loader.late_tls` holds only a program with no other TLS module, index 1, and Microsoft's PE format page says only that the loader writes the index at AddressOfIndex. Settled by a late_tls probe row from the Windows runner: a program importing a TLS DLL at the start that loads a second TLS DLL and reads its index, not by asking.
- **2026-10-29 (reviewer-3's project review, Medium 3)** — Where does v0.2's product slice go in the plan? v0.2 §1 and §12.1 make the product the one-command operation (resolve the Steam distribution, obtain it under the user's account, prepare it, establish its Steam session, run, preserve state, relaunch), §4.2 the Steam bridge a first-class subsystem, §4.3 the output a Mac application bundle; `docs/rust-port-plan-2026-10-24.md` runs steps 1-10 to milestone C with no step for them, and nothing records the deferral. The plan is the user's; the question is theirs: a step 11 after milestone C, a thread beside steps 5-10, or another place. — **Answered 2026-10-29 (user):** more milestones, after C, unless something must change now; nothing must, by lookup. The Decision "Milestones for the product slice, after milestone C" gives milestones D (the product slice) and E (the Steam session).
- **2026-10-29 (reviewer-3's refreshed project review, direction)** — Milestone E's gate (plan step 12) holds the generated steamclient64.dll to a capture of the game's Steam calls made on Windows. The Windows laptop is expected, not yet here. Is the capture made on the laptop, or by the Windows runner the family probes use? Revisit when milestone D's gate is met, or on the laptop's arrival, whichever comes first.

### Sprint 339 (2026-11-25, closed-partial)
- **Scope:** the host design's Change 1, its None class: nine services (GetCurrentThreadId, GetCurrentProcessId, TlsGetValue, TlsSetValue, FlsGetValue, FlsSetValue, GetLastError, SetLastError, QueryPerformanceCounter) to take no runtime word in `guest::bridge`, after a proof that each touches only its thread's or vCPU's own state; closed partial at the proof, which failed for all nine
- **Dual contract:** signal — none; no source changed. Artifact pass (`scripts/gates.sh` exit 0 in 196 s, kept in `store/gates/passes.tsv` as sprint 339 at 6db5fcdfd with root=9fffa122d, the tree Sprint 338 left, and "rust=crates python=0 samples=0", base=cc2c67578, Sprint 338's commit; multi-vCPU: not run (Python gates not run)). Observation: the proof, quoted line by line in the card. The bridge's path between `take_runtime` and `release_runtime` (`guest.rs` 577-581) stores nothing, its other stores landing in the vCPU's own pages. Every one of the nine returns through `w2m_logged` (`tools/guest_runtime/runtime.c` 60-70), which writes the process-wide call ring at `PROCESS_VA + RING_OFF` by unlocked load, add, store, the built `_w2m_logged` reloading `count` before each field's store: on two vCPUs counts are lost and a record can be written one past the ring, into the exception area at `PROCESS_VA + 0x45000`. Apart from the ring: GetCurrentThreadId, GetCurrentProcessId, GetLastError, SetLastError, TlsGetValue and TlsSetValue touch only the thread's TEB and slots; FlsGetValue reads a block FlsAlloc and FlsFree write on every thread; FlsSetValue's first set takes from the process heap and links `fls_blocks` (`thread_storage.c` 46, 50-51); QueryPerformanceCounter's clock mode 1 writes the transport page (`runtime.c` 26-35) and mode 2 reads a shared page. Plants 1 and 2 reached no judge, the lock class not built, their replacement the built `_w2m_logged`'s unlocked update quoted in the card.
- **CI does not run:** nothing changed for it to run.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:** *Sequence narration:* no run, no record. *Observation 1 (a premise the design stated and the code did not hold):* the design's None class, "for what one thread owns or what no one writes" (`docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-24.md` 105), missed `w2m_logged`, which every service shares; the card's proof-first rule caught it before any change. Disposition: recorded, the Decision "Sprint 339's None class stopped at its proof" and Deferred (Sprint 339, partial). *Observation 2 (a Signal Report blind to its own record, not met here):* card 339 made no record, so the generator's blind spot (the eleventh review's High 1) does not reach this close. Disposition: recorded.
- **Closed:** Sprint 338's CI run 45740425128 (success on cc2c67578, Sprint 338's commit), kept in `store/gates/ci.tsv` with its workflow, ci.
- **Files:** sprints/sprint-339-the-thread-local-calls-take-no-runtime-word.md (the result, the proof), store/gates/ (ci.tsv, passes.tsv, steps.tsv, timing.tsv), BLACKBOARD.md (Surfaced reviewer-4's eleventh review, Decisions, Deferred (Sprint 339, partial) and the eleventh review's Low 1, Built 329), docs/sprint-tail-archive-2026-10-08.md (Sprint 329's entry), signal-reports/sprint-339-report.md.

### Sprint 338 (2026-11-25, closed)
- **Scope:** C1's first card, the host design's Change 1 begun (the Decision "Step 6 restored to the user's fix (b)"): the host's take and release of the runtime word in `run.rs` behind one guard, `RuntimeWordGuard`, released in its `Drop` when the host took the word; `take_word`, `take_word_unchecked` and `turn` carry it, and the nine service sites drop it where they released by hand; no service's order or locks changed
- **Dual contract:** signal — none, how the host holds the word; artifact pass (`scripts/gates.sh` exit 0 in 366 s, kept in `store/gates/passes.tsv` as sprint 338 at ba2883845 with root=9fffa122d and "rust=crates python=0 samples=0", 224 tests of `rdeps(w2m_vm)`, base=fa47573ca, Sprint 337's commit; multi-vCPU: not run (Python gates not run)); observation pass (`host_release_runtime_word` in `run.rs`'s code 11 before and 1 after, the guard's `Drop`, counted by the supervisor; `run_loop::the_runtime_word_is_free_after_each_served_exit`, `sleeper` on 1 and 2 vCPUs reading `RUNTIME_LOCK` 0 after its stop, its reach read before the change (the turn's start, an exit's diagnostics, `hvc #1`, `hvc #12` and a vCPU taking a thread); `run::tests::the_word_is_released_only_by_its_guards_drop`; `run::tests::a_stopped_runs_take_of_the_word_ends_its_service`; the three passing, rerun by the supervisor; plant 1, the `Drop` releasing nothing, "sleeper on 1 vCPUs did not end in 120 s: a take waits on a runtime word left held" and "the word released by the guard's drop left: 4294967295 right: 0", the second rerun by the supervisor)
- **CI does not run:** the VM tests (macOS); CI builds the change on Linux and runs the text test.
- **Rubber Duck Pass:** *Sequence narration:* no record (the card emits no tags). *Observation 1 (timing surprise):* under plant 1 the run outlived its own 60-s limit, its vCPUs spinning in `host_take_runtime_word`, which reads no stop, in host code the limit does not reach. Disposition: deferred, Deferred (Sprint 338) (a), C5. *Observation 2 (order violation, new with the guard):* a service that panics now releases the host's hold as it unwinds, before the run's stop is set, so a peer may take the word and serve one exit on state the panic left half written; before, the peer spun for ever and the run hung at its join. The panic still propagates through the scope's join, so no run passes with it. Disposition: deferred, Deferred (Sprint 338) (b). *Observation 3 (a test's own page):* `a_stopped_runs_take_of_the_word_ends_its_service` failed once after the change: its guest's page had no `LOCK_OWNER`, so `own` read 0 and storing 0 freed the word; the test now writes the owner and asserts it. Disposition: resolved-here. *Observation 4 (the close refused once):* `signal_report 338 --check` refused the prepared close, "its observation contract names a planted fault and its result quotes no judge's failure for a plant": the supervisor's card wrote "Planted fault:" singular, so `numbered_plants` (which looks for "Planted faults") found none and the unnumbered rule wanted a quote on a line naming a plant, while the result's "Plant 1" line held none, its quote on the next; the bullet rewrapped, the quote on its first line, and the check passed. Disposition: resolved-here; the card form recorded, "Planted faults" with numbered plants, as cards 335-337 wrote.
- **Closed:** Sprint 337's CI run 45737611682 (success on fa47573ca, Sprint 337's commit), kept in `store/gates/ci.tsv` with its workflow, ci.

Every tag and value in 0.13. *Observation 1 (payload surprise):* `guest_wfe_exit` reads absent on this Mac: a guest's WFE does not trap to the host and a WFI does; 0.13's note says "a guest WFE or WFI traps", the card's rule took the WFE run.
